# CI flakes

Every failure of `pipeline.yml` that passed on a re-run, with its root cause and its fix, so the next failure of the
same test starts from what is known. How to investigate one: skill `ci-failure` (`.claude/skills/ci-failure/`), with
`eng/ci-failures.sh <run id>` and `eng/ci-failures.sh --recent 100`. A row whose fix didn't hold gets the new run and
what it showed; it isn't replaced.

The first sweep (2026-10-27) read every failed job of the last 100 runs, 2026-10-23 to 2026-10-27, in every attempt:
about 45 failed jobs, 30 distinct failures, of which only a handful were the pull request's own.

**M65's stress (2026-11-19)**, for the maintainer's rule for 1.0: a row neither reproduced nor reasoned to its cause
closes after 50 passes under heavy load with its diagnostic in place, and reopens if it fails again. The CI stress run
44465663261 (a scratch branch on develop `9829a094`, never merged) ran each open row's tests in a loop on the runners
they failed on, Windows, Linux x64 and arm64 and macOS, with half of each runner's cores kept busy (2 busy loops on 4
cores, on 3 on macOS) and the group's test projects at once: 30 jobs, no failure. The Linux rows' tests also ran on
WSL (20 cores, Release, their projects at once, 55 rounds) and the web rows' on a Windows PC (two loops at once).

## Tests

| Failure | Where | Runs | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DiffInsightsTests.A_working_tree_diff_has_Quick_Info_and_CodeLens_without_the_file_open`: "Timed out waiting for the shell" for the CodeLens row above the changed `Radius` line (`DiffInsightsTests.cs:92`), after the row above `IShape` had come | Local WSL validation of the 1.0 release candidate (2026-11-19), Shell tests under heavy load (2h 13m against the usual 40 minutes, another validation sharing the machine) | local | The product's. A diff resolves its CodeLens counts near what it shows (`DiffInsights.ResolveAsync`), and a resolve that failed (the language service busy or not ready, a `JsonRpcRemoteException`) was dropped with "the next scroll or refresh asks again". Nothing asks while the diff stays put, so every lens after the failed one kept a blank row until the user scrolled. The editor's lenses retry a failure (`EditorInsights.RetryAfterFailure`); the diff's didn't. Reasoned from the code and reproduced by failing resolves on purpose: three failed resolves in a row left every row blank, the run's symptom | A failed resolve is asked again, up to five times, as the editor's are; a cancellation (a newer resolve) isn't a failure. The test fails three resolves on purpose and checks every lens resolves anyway (`DiffInsights.FailingResolvesForTests`): it fails without the fix. Its wait for `Radius`'s row says the row, the line's lenses (resolved or not) and the last resolve failure. It then passed 50 of 50 on Windows runners under load in the CI stress run. **Failed again (2026-11-19), the 1.0 validation in WSL** (the release candidate, the full suite under load): the new check timed out with lenses 30 to 47 never resolved ("unresolved (shown '')") and 4 to 26 resolved. The test's, not the product's: only the lenses near the lines the diff shows are resolved, and the file with its lens rows is taller than the pane, so the check that every lens resolves held only when an earlier scroll had shown the rest. **Fixed:** the check is of the lenses near the shown lines (`DiffInsights.IsNearShown`), IShape's among them, and its message marks the others. |
| `BuildIntegrationTests.Run_info_and_launch_profiles_start_the_built_program`, `ProjectManagementIntegrationTests.Unloaded_projects_are_remembered_skipped_by_builds_and_reloaded` and `BuildDepthIntegrationTests.Batch_builds_build_each_configuration_and_skip_up_to_date_ones`: "Build canceled" after 2:10 to 2:15 (the first two; Greetings, or Core for both its frameworks, built), and in the batch a Debug build of 2:48 then its Release build canceled after 8 s | Windows, the 1.0 release candidate's local run (2026-11-19, develop 9314aaaf, the whole suite 3 modules at a time, beside another session's Shell tests: CPU about 91%) | not CI | The tests': each build ran out the test's own limit, every call's in its class (`Timeout()`: 2 minutes a call in the first two classes, 3 for the whole batch of two builds); "Elapsed" runs 10 to 15 s past it while the cancellation ends the worker nodes. The builds were slow because the machine was, not for anything users would hit on a machine that isn't: measured with MSBuild's debug logs (`MSBUILDDEBUGENGINE`, a binary log of every build) in two runs of the whole module on this machine at 90–97% from other sessions. samples/BuildAndRun's two-project build takes 3 to 4 s idle and took up to 60 s there; NuGet's restore task stayed under 1 s in every build (no slow restore); the time spread over every target: evaluations, the restore's graph walk, CoreCompile, and the scheduler's round trips between projects, which pass through the central node, the build host (from a project's MSBuild task to its last child's end and back: p90 0.6 s against 15 ms idle). Nothing waited or ran one at a time in the host: the CLI's own builds of the same sample (`dotnet build -nr:false -m`, fresh nodes as the host's), looped at the same time, took as long as the host's builds that started in the same minute (39 s against 44 to 60 s at 03:01, 23 s against 17 to 27 s at 03:02, 3 s against 2 to 3 s once the load fell) with the same round trips. Reproduced on the old code: the whole module under the same load with 20 busy loops beside failed six builds this way (these three, `A_build_can_be_cancelled_and_only_one_runs_at_a_time`'s second build, `ItemsIntegrationTests.A_folder_opens_without_projects_then_loads_builds_and_remembers_them` at its 2 minutes, and `DotnetVersionsTests`' net11 build at its 3: that row below). | **Closed (2026-11-19).** Builds get a limit of their own, 5 minutes a build as the module's other build tests have (`BuildLimit.Token`, the batch 5 a build), in every class whose limit was shorter (`BuildIntegrationTests`, `BuildDepthIntegrationTests`, `ProjectManagementIntegrationTests`, `ItemsIntegrationTests`, `PackageIntegrationTests`, `CustomToolIntegrationTests`, `DotnetVersionsTests`, `WindowsDesktopTests`); the other calls keep their class's. Under the same load (20 busy loops, other sessions at 90%) the whole module then passed, 221 tests, where the old code failed 6. |
| `BuildHostIntegrationTests.A_result_built_from_an_import_cached_before_it_changed_is_not_served_for_the_new_file`: "Timed out." (`BuildHostIntegrationTests.cs:911`, after 90 s), the wait for B's design-time build with the import as MSBuild had cached it (BEFORE) | Windows, a run of the whole `Codalus.Projects.Tests` on a machine at 90–97% from other sessions, with MSBuild's debug logs on (`MSBUILDDEBUGENGINE`), M65 (2026-11-19) | not CI | Not known: the wait said nothing of what B got. The test sets its case up through MSBuild's cache: B's evaluation must take the changed import from the cache, as A's read it. If the old one had left the cache by then (it keeps the last 50 files read for sure and the others only while nothing collects them, and every evaluation reads many SDK files), B reads the new file (AFTER) and the wait for BEFORE runs out; or B wasn't evaluated again at all. It passed in the module's 3 runs after, two of them under 20 busy loops. | Open. Diagnostic: the wait's failure names B's defines (BEFORE, AFTER or none), the design-time progress and the host's last 30 log lines, which tell the two apart. **Closed (2026-11-19), not reproduced, under the maintainer's rule for 1.0** (M65's stress, above): 55 of 55 passed on Windows (20 logical cores) at develop `eb5cee0a`, beside other sessions' test suites and builds with the CPU at 100%, each in 4.5 to 10.1 s. The diagnostic stays. |
| `XamlPreviewerTests`' three tests (`A_window_renders_updates_on_edit_and_reports_errors`, `User_controls_and_library_resources_render_with_design_time_data`, `The_agent_hit_tests_finds_bounds_and_switches_themes_and_scale`) all failed at 0 ms | Windows, a local run of all of `Codalus.Workbench.Tests` (2026-11-18, branch `claude/merge-align-reindent`), while another worktree's test run used the same machine; the class passed 3 of 3 alone right after, and the whole project passed on the next run | not CI | **Open.** Not known: the run kept only the failed tests' names, not their messages. Three tests at 0 ms is their class fixture failing (`AvaloniaFixture.InitializeAsync`): it copies `samples/Avalonia` to a temp folder and builds it, and a failed build fails its `Assert.True(code == 0, output)` | None needed to find it: the fixture's assertion carries the build's output, so the next failure says why. Keep the messages of a local run's failures |
| `XamlLanguageTests.Renaming_a_user_control_class_in_csharp_renames_x_Class_and_its_elements`: `avalonia-rename-x-class-from-csharp.txt` differs from the result, `MainWindow.axaml`'s hunk at `@@ 30` instead of `@@ 28` (`XamlGolden.cs:51`) | Windows, a local run of `tests/Codalus.LanguageServices.Host.Tests` (2026-11-19) | local | The test's. The class's tests share one language host and its open documents, and `Elements_attributes_and_values_complete` types two lines into `MainWindow.axaml`'s open text. The rename test opened only the code-behind fresh, so when that test ran before it (the order of the whole class's run), the rename's edit to `<views:TodoItemView />` was rendered against the edited window, two lines down. Every other refactoring test that reaches `MainWindow.axaml` puts its text back first (`FreshAsync`). Not the product: the edit is right for the text it was computed on. | The test calls `FreshAsync(MainWindow)` too. Run with `Elements_attributes_and_values_complete` alone (`--filter-method` for both), it fails without the line and passes with it. |
| `AcpAgentTests.A_crash_mid_turn_ends_the_turn_with_the_agents_last_words_and_the_next_question_starts_it_again`: `Assert.Equal` expected "Starting…", the turn's answer was empty (`AcpAgentTests.cs:513`) | macOS `all`, `develop`'s nightly (2026-11-18) | 44259758111 | The product's, in `JsonRpcConnection.WhenDispatchedAsync`: the stand-in agent sends "Starting…" and exits. The end of the input fails the turn's `session/prompt` as soon as it is read, ahead of the update read before it, so `AcpClient.PromptAsync`, which waits for the updates read before an answer, didn't wait on a failed turn; and `WhenDispatchedAsync` itself returned at once after the end, its marker refused by the channel the end had completed, though what was read before the end could still be queued. On a loaded runner the turn ended before the update was handled, and users lost an agent's last words the same way. | `WhenDispatchedAsync` waits for the dispatch loop to drain after the end, and `PromptAsync` waits for the updates on a failed turn too. `JsonRpcConnectionTests.When_dispatched_after_the_end_waits_for_the_notifications_read_before_it` (three notifications with a slow handler, then the end) fails without the fix (none handled) and passes with it; `AcpAgentTests.A_crash_right_after_many_updates_keeps_every_one_of_them` checks the whole path. |
| No failed test: `[T4 host] restart failed: Cannot access a disposed object` in `CustomToolIntegrationTests`' output, after `Text_templates_run_in_the_T4_host_with_includes_and_report_errors` kills the host | Every OS, the test's own log (it passes) | none (a log line) | The product's: `TextTemplatingService` took a host that wasn't running for a stale one, disposed its supervisor and started a new host, while the supervisor was restarting the one that crashed (its 250 ms backoff, then its start). That restart then met its disposed supervisor (the start lock, or the launch ended after the dispose, which kills what it started) and logged its failure. A user whose template ran in that second got two hosts started, one killed at once, and the misleading line in the T4 log | The service keeps its supervisor and asks it to start (`SupervisedProcess.StartAsync` waits for a restart under way, or starts one that gave up); a supervisor disposed during a restart doesn't log it as failed. `CustomToolIntegrationTests.A_template_sent_while_the_host_restarts_after_a_crash_waits_for_that_restart` sends a template as soon as the supervisor sees the kill and fails without the fix (the host launched twice). Validated on Windows |
| `ComposeProjectTests.A_dcproj_loads_its_compose_files_and_the_solution_builds_without_it` (and, in the same run, `ProjectManagementIntegrationTests.Setting_properties_writes_the_minimal_change`: the next row) | Windows, the same loaded local run of the whole suite as the T4 row below (M65, 2026-11-17); it passed alone. Again 2026-11-19, a run of the whole `Codalus.Projects.Tests` on a machine at 90–97% from other sessions, with MSBuild's debug logs on (`MSBUILDDEBUGENGINE`), message kept | not CI | Not known on 2026-11-17 (the run kept only the names; not reproduced in 6 runs of the classes under 20 busy cores). **Found (2026-11-19):** "The build could not run: The process cannot access the file '…\Containers\.codalus\state\Containers.slnx\build.slnf' because it is being used by another process." The product's: a build of a solution that leaves projects out (a compose project, unloaded projects, projects that need the classic IDE's targets) writes a filter of those it builds, `build.slnf`, in the solution's state folder (`BuildEntryPoint`), before it waits for the restore that opening started (`ExclusiveBuildAsync`). That restore is a `dotnet restore` of the same filter, which opening wrote, and reads it while it runs: on Windows the build's write of the same file failed with a sharing violation, and the user's build didn't run. A restore started while another one runs (`StartRestore` writes its entry point at once), and a folder's generated `folder.slnx`, had the same race. | **Closed (2026-11-19).** `GeneratedSolutionFile.Write`, for `build.slnf`, `restore.slnf` and the folder solutions: the same text, as it nearly always is, isn't written again; a changed one (a project unloaded meanwhile) retries a sharing violation for a few seconds, as `ProjectXml.SaveFile` does. `GeneratedSolutionFileTests` (a reader holding the filter as a restore does: the same text written again; a changed text while the reader lets go after 300 ms) fail without it with the run's message and pass with it. The test passed in the three loaded runs of the module after it (Windows; two of them with 20 busy loops beside). |
| `ProjectManagementIntegrationTests.Setting_properties_writes_the_minimal_change` | Windows, the same loaded local run of the whole suite as the T4 row below (M65, 2026-11-17); it passed alone | not CI | Not known: the run kept only the failed test's name. Not reproduced: 6 runs of 6 under 20 busy cores (2026-11-17), and 4 loaded runs of the whole module (2026-11-19, 90–100% CPU, two of them with 20 busy loops beside). Ruled out by reading: a sharing violation saving the project file while an evaluation reads it (`ProjectXml.SaveFile` retries it on Windows for 4.5 s). Ruled out by a probe (2026-11-19): the test's own write of Core.csproj (`File.WriteAllText`, line 161, no retry) could fail the same way if the host read Core then, but nothing held Core.csproj open in the 2 to 19 s from the open to that write (polled every 2 ms for a writer's access, 4 runs, one under the load above). Not the build limit of the row above either: the test builds nothing. | Open. The assertions already say what they saw (the project file's text and each property's value and origin), so the next failure needs only its message kept: run validations with their whole output (`dotnet test … > log`). **Closed (2026-11-19), not reproduced, under the maintainer's rule for 1.0** (M65's stress, above): 50 of 50 passed on Windows runners in the CI stress run. The assertions still say what they saw. |
| `CustomToolIntegrationTests.Text_templates_run_in_the_T4_host_with_includes_and_report_errors`, printing "T4 host exited with exit code -1" and "restart failed: Cannot access a disposed object" (`SupervisedProcess`) | Windows, a loaded local run of the whole suite (M65, 2026-11-17); it passed alone | not CI | The product's. The test kills the T4 host, waits 500 ms and runs a template, which a host that dies must survive. `TextTemplatingService` reused the host's connection while `IsRunning` said so, and that stays true until the connection notices the loss (its read ends, `OnDisconnected`): on a loaded machine that took longer than 500 ms, so the template went to the dead host and came back "The T4 host exited while running the template." (no output). The log's two lines are the kill's exit code (-1 is `Process.Kill` on Windows) and the supervisor's own restart, whose process the test's disposal had already disposed: not an error of their own. Forced by running the template at once after the kill: it failed 3 runs of 3 with that result. | Fixed: a transform whose connection is lost runs once more, on a new host (`ConnectionAsync` never hands back the connection it just lost); a template that ends its host itself ends the second one too and is reported as before. The test now runs the template at once after the kill (fails without the fix, 2 of 2) and runs a template that calls `Environment.Exit`. The class passed 3 runs of 3 |
| `CodeSearchTests.Code_search_filters_ranks_previews_edits_and_opens_results`: `Assert.Equal` failed for the saved scope, "currentDocument" expected, "entireSolution" read (`CodeSearchTests.cs:151`) | Linux CI (`test (ubuntu-latest, shell-1)`), PR #443 (an adapter change: nothing in the shell) | 44075755320 | The test's. Setting the window's scope saves it in the background (`ShellHost.OnOptionsChanged` starts `SettingsService.SetAsync`, which writes the settings file and then reads it again); the test's search, in one document's text, ended before the write, and it read the setting at once. Forced locally (Windows): holding the settings file open while the scope changes and the search runs gives the CI's message exactly | The test waits for the saved setting (with the setting and the window's scope in the message on a timeout). The class passed 4 of 4 on Windows. **The product bug it showed, fixed (2026-11-17):** forcing it showed that a file kept locked through all 20 of the write's retries (1 s) lost the change without a word, as the scope is saved without waiting (`_ = settings.SetAsync(…)`, like most of the shell's settings). `SettingsService` now keeps a change it couldn't write (in effect, applied to what each reload reads), raises `WriteFailed` (the workbench shows a warning naming the file, and takes it back when written) and writes it with the file's next change or a retry (after 1 s, doubling to 30 s, and once more on exit). `SettingsServiceTests.A_change_its_locked_file_refuses_is_reported_kept_and_written_later` and `The_workbench_warns_while_a_settings_change_waits_for_its_file` fail without it (the `IOException` the save threw) |

| `EditingDepthTests.Snippets_insert_with_fields_surround_selections_and_come_from_completion_and_imports`: "Timed out waiting for the shell" (`EditingDepthTests.cs:308`) after 1 min 37 s: in 90 s the completion window never held the `cw` snippet after typing `cw` and asking for completion | macOS CI (`test (macos-latest, all)`, `shell-2`), PR #427 (nothing in the shell) | 44030324314 | **Found (2026-11-19), the product's.** Reproduced on Windows under load (every core busy for 100 to 700 ms, then idle as long, beside another agent's test runs and a WSL test run): 4 of 30 runs, each with the window opened from the one request (42 items) and closed at once. A log of the popup's close and of every scroll said what closed it: typing `cw` on the editor's last line scrolled it to the bottom edge (offset 646, the line's bottom at 1051 of a 405 px view), two CodeLens rows (17.5 px each) then arrived above it for the members the test had just added and pushed it to 1069 to 1087, below the edge, without a scroll; then the scroll viewer set the horizontal offset the text view had asked for (15 px) back to 0. `OnPopupViewScrolled` closed the popups on any scroll that found the caret's line out of view, so that sideways scroll closed the list as if the user had scrolled the line away. Whether the correction came before the list opened (it passed) or after (it failed) was the timing. Before that: Typing each letter asks for completion and Ctrl+Space asks again, each request cancelling the one before; the last one's window lists every snippet. The paths that show nothing are a missing C# client, a cancelled request, a window already open (then the request is dropped), and the caret having moved away meanwhile, which was the one not logged. The shard's console says nothing of the shell, and the wait said nothing of the window | Diagnostic: the wait says whether a window is open, with how many items from where and the first of them, the caret and the text before it, the C# service's state and client, the snippets known, and the Language Services log; the caret-moved path now logs too (`completion not shown (the caret moved away meanwhile …)`). Open until it fails again; it passed 7 runs of 7 locally (Windows) **Fix:** the popups close only when a scroll up or down takes the caret's line out of view; a scroll that doesn't move the text up or down places them again (`CodeEditorController.OnPopupViewScrolled`). `IntelliSensePopupTests.Popups_close_when_a_scroll_up_or_down_takes_the_caret_line_away_not_on_a_sideways_one` opens the list on the bottom line, takes the line out of view without a scroll and scrolls sideways: the list closed without the fix and stays with it; a scroll up still closes it. Still so: a CodeLens row arriving above the caret's line can push it below the bottom edge (the list now stays, placed against it). **Closed (2026-11-19).** With the fix, 50 of 50 runs passed on Windows 11 (20 logical cores), each run one process with the tests of six rows of this ledger (EditingDepthTests' snippets, EditorTests' signature help, XamlShellTests' previewer, EverydayResponsivenessTests' Git Repository flows, RemoteShellTests' remote window, SettingsScrollTests' rows), under load that comes and goes (every core busy for 100 to 700 ms, then idle as long) beside other agents' shell test runs and builds on the same machine (a run took 2 to 8 minutes, the Git Repository flows alone up to 131 s instead of about 40). |
| `ComposeShellTests.Debug_compose_debugs_every_dotnet_service_restarts_one_alone_and_takes_an_edit` and `ComposeShellTests.A_compose_service_in_its_container_is_profiled_through_its_diagnostic_port` failed instead of skipping: compose's "Error while fetching server API version: ('Connection aborted.', FileNotFoundError(2, …))"; with them `ContainerShellTests.A_project_runs_and_debugs_in_a_container_through_its_container_profile`: "Timed out waiting for the shell" at the editor's debug line (`ContainerShellTests.cs:166`) | Local WSL validation (Ubuntu with podman-docker: podman 4.9.3 behind the `docker` command, docker-compose 1.29.2), 2026-11-17 | none (local) | The compose tests': CLAUDE.md says a test skips locally without its prerequisite, and their check (`ContainerShellTests.RequireEngineAsync`) asked only whether the engine's CLI answers. Under podman-docker `docker version` answers through rootless podman, while `docker compose` hands over to docker-compose 1.29, which talks to Docker's API socket (`/var/run/docker.sock`, a link to the system podman socket, not running) and fails at its first command. The container test is another failure: its prerequisite holds (the Ctrl+F5 half ran and answered in its container under podman), and the debug half stopped at the breakpoint with no source in any frame ("Greeting.For at :0 \| Program.<>c.<<Main>$>b__0_0 at :0 \| [External Code]"), so the editor never showed the line. Why the Codalus debugger in a rootless podman container has no lines isn't known yet; CI's Docker passes it | The compose tests need their compose to reach the engine too (`ContainerShellTests.RequireComposeEngineAsync`: a `compose ps` of a project that doesn't exist, which reads the engine's API and changes nothing), skipping locally and failing on CI's Linux runner as before; both skip under WSL now with compose's own error as the reason. Diagnostic for the container test: the wait says the stop, the top frames with their sources and lines, the editor and the Debug Console. Open for the container test under podman. **Root cause of the container test (2026-11-19), the product's:** not podman. The Codalus debugger runs in the container (copied there), and gave a frame its source only when the PDB's document was on its own disk (`File.Exists` in `NetFxDebugSession.StackTrace`). A program built here has its sources here: in the container `/tmp/codalus-shell-…/Web/Program.cs` isn't there (only the build output is mounted, at `/app`), so every frame came back without a source and line 0, in a container or on any other machine, for every user with the bundles a published install carries. Breakpoints bound, as they match the PDB's documents by name. CI passed only because the shard that runs `ContainerShellTests` (Linux `shell-1`) doesn't build the Codalus debugger's bundle, so `RemoteDebuggers` fell back to netcoredbg there, which doesn't check; the WSL mirror has the bundle (`eng/validate.sh` fetches it). **Fixed (2026-11-19):** a remote session's `launch` and `attach` say the sources are the client's (`NetFxDebugArguments.SourcesOnClient`, set by `DebugService` for the Codalus debugger on another machine or in a container), and then a frame names its PDB's document and line whatever is on the debugger's disk; the client maps the path as it does netcoredbg's. Locally nothing changes. `ClrDebuggerTests.Frames_name_their_document_and_line_when_the_sources_are_the_clients_though_not_on_the_debuggers_disk` moves the program's source away and fails without the fix ("Expected ("Program.cs", 73), Actual (null, 0)"). The container test passed 6 of 6 under rootless podman in WSL afterwards; it had failed in each of the three runs before the fix. Found on the way: in `eng/wsl-test.sh`'s mirror, which has no commit, `eng/get-codalus-debugger.sh` took a stale bundle for up to date after an edit (its id read only HEAD and a diff against it), so the first rerun still ran the old adapter; the id now reads the index's blobs too. Left for CI: its Linux shards that run the container and compose tests still debug in them with netcoredbg, as they have no bundle. |

The build host reports each project as it's evaluated (`ProjectEvaluated`), and `get_solution` lists the projects evaluated so far; the test's wait ended as soon as the text had `- Hello`, before Greetings was evaluated. An agent reading the list while the solution loads took a part of it for all of it, with nothing to say otherwise | The test waits for both projects. `get_solution` says `Loading: n of m projects evaluated so far` while the solution's projects aren't all evaluated, and the test checks that every read it made without both projects said so, and the last one doesn't. | `XamlShellTests.The_previewer_follows_the_editor_and_selects_both_ways`: "Timed out waiting for the shell." (`XamlShellTests.cs:140`), the 60 s wait for the 2× frame after **Scale** was set to `2×`; passed alone right after | Local Windows run of all of `Codalus.Shell.Tests` (56 min), while a WSL test run, a Release build and a shell on Xvfb ran on the same machine; branch `claude/bottom-tab-single-click` | none (local) | Not known at first: the wait said nothing of the previewer's state, so the run doesn't tell whether the 2× frame never came, came at another size, or the previewer errored | Diagnostic: every wait of that test after the first frame now says the previewer's stage, status, frame count, its log and the build's output; the 2× wait also the last frame's size and scale, the selected scale and the error. Next failure: read them **Reproduced (2026-11-19)** on Windows, 1 of 22 runs of the test in one process with the tests of five other rows, under load that comes and goes on every core beside other agents' test runs and builds: "the last frame 480x360 at 1, scale "2×", error ""; stage Ready … frames 7". So the previewer ran, the theme's frame had come, no error, and after Scale nothing more came for 60 s. **The cause, the product's:** Avalonia's designer queues what it sends for a worker that can miss its wake-up (`TransportConnectionWrapper`: the worker finds the queue empty, a send comes before it waits again, and the message stays queued until the designer sends anything else). The session already worked around it for an update's answer (it sends the update again), but not for a frame: after Scale (and after the update that follows it, and a theme) the frame can wait there, nothing else comes, and the update took its 10 s wait for a frame for "nothing changed" and kept the last one. Users could see it: the preview keeping the old scale or theme until the next edit. **Fix:** after an update, a new scale or a theme, when no frame comes within a second the session asks the designer to measure the viewport at the size the last frame shows, which changes nothing and is answered at once, and the answer sends what waited before it (`PreviewerSession.FlushHeldFrameSoon`). The stand-in designer now holds frames as the real one can: `PreviewProtocolTests.A_frame_the_designer_holds_back_comes_when_the_session_asks_it_for_an_answer` got the previous frame for the update after 10 s without the fix, and its frame within a second, and the new scale's frame, with it; `XamlPreviewerTests` (the real designer) pass. **Closed (2026-11-19).** Before the fix 21 of 22 runs passed, with the fix 28 of 28, on Windows 11 (20 logical cores), each run one process with the tests of six rows of this ledger (EditingDepthTests' snippets, EditorTests' signature help, XamlShellTests' previewer, EverydayResponsivenessTests' Git Repository flows, RemoteShellTests' remote window, SettingsScrollTests' rows), under load that comes and goes (every core busy for 100 to 700 ms, then idle as long) beside other agents' shell test runs and builds on the same machine (a run took 2 to 8 minutes, the Git Repository flows alone up to 131 s instead of about 40). |

So all that time the caller's thread stood right after the call, the task only in the call's return register: no continuation on the task, no awaiter of it in a field or a local, and it isn't in the method or its kickoff (#363's and the held case's ways). | `RuntimeAsyncDebuggingTests.Into_the_program_from_the_library_built_the_other_way_and_out_again(AsyncDebuggee)`: "No 'stopped' event" after Step Out; the program printed `async total=16` and exited | Linux (x64, a cloud session), the full `eng/validate.sh` of 2026-11-14 (the daily dependency updates, nothing near the debugger), then 1 in 12 runs of the class beside four busy loops; 20 of 20 alone | not CI | The product's, in M61's async Step Out (ADR 0100), seen in the adapter's log (`CODALUS_CLR_DEBUGGER_LOG`). In this build the program's callback (`TripleAsync`, a state machine) is awaited by the runtime-async library, so `StepOutOfResumed` finds no awaiting state machine and falls back to a Just My Code step on the stepping thread, with no breakpoints in the program's `RoundTripAsync`, which awaits the library. Passing, the library completed and `RoundTripAsync` resumed on that thread (three step callbacks, then the stop). Failing, the thread pool started a thread just after the step began, and the library's completion and `RoundTripAsync`'s resumption ran where the stepper isn't: no step callback came again and the program ran to its end. Users can hit it: a Step Out of a callback that runtime-async code awaits runs on when the awaiting code resumes on another thread. | Open. A fix needs the state machine that awaits the runtime-async call's task: the runtime doesn't expose a call's continuation (the `TODO(issue)` in `StepOutOfResumed`), so the adapter would have to find it some other way (the user's state machines awaiting a task the call completes) or say that the step can't follow. Left to the async-stepping work (M61); this row has the evidence. **Closed as a documented limit, not a flake:** ADR 0100 records it (the state-machine callback awaited by the runtime-async library) with its `TODO(issue)`, and since PR #359 the test accepts either of the limit's outcomes (the row of run 43526072431), so it can't fail on it; CI has run them on every OS since without this failure: the 0.14.0 release run (43954624197: Linux x64 and arm64, Windows, macOS) and the last 100 runs (`eng/ci-failures.sh --recent 100`, 2026-11-17) |

Nothing explains it yet: not seen again in the 180 pipeline runs from 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-17 (78 with Windows' `others` shard passing), nor in 40 local runs on Windows under load that comes and goes. | `GitChangesRowsTests.A_status_read_that_changes_nothing_changes_no_row_and_a_hidden_window_builds_no_tree`: "Assert.False() Failure" at `Assert.False(vm.IsRefreshing)` (line 457), right after setting "the same Git LFS state again" | Windows shell-1, PR #284's run (the build host's string-cache gate: nothing near Git Changes) | 41549374686 | The test's. Not the assignment: the repository has no LFS, so the shell's own read set `GitLfsStatus.None`, the value the test sets, and an equal state refreshes nothing. But `IsRefreshing` is also set from other threads: a status read's `StatusChanged` posts a refresh from wherever the read ran. The test's `File.Delete` a few steps earlier is a changed file's event, which comes after the test's own read of it has begun (`_lastRead` is taken before git runs), so the watcher reads again 400 ms or more later and raises `StatusChanged`, posting a refresh at a moment the test doesn't control: on a slow runner, between the last wait and the assertion. Measured here under CPU load with a fresh cache each time: 5.5 s and 2.6 s with the cmdlets, 1.7 s and 1.3 s for the same program with .NET's calls; the runner's slower disk and larger module set make the 30 s plausible, and it matches what was seen (PowerShell running, no pid file). | `HostRuntimeSetupTests.A_language_host_that_exits_at_startup_is_a_start_failure_not_a_crash_and_is_tried_again_on_request`: the test watchdog's hang report after 10 minutes | Linux `others`, PR #321's run (.NET 11 GA) | 42964724644 | In the product, through .NET. The connection's read loop spun in `SafePipeHandle.CreatePipeSocket` → `Socket.Dispose` → `SafeSocketHandle.CloseAsIs`: on Unix a redirected process stream is a pipe stream that makes its socket on its first read or write, and the host, exiting at startup, was disposed at that moment. `CreatePipeSocket` then closes the new socket while it still holds the pipe's handle, and the socket's close waits for that handle's release forever. A small program (`Process` with redirected output, a first `ReadAsync` and a dispose at once) hangs 3 times in 400 on the 11.0 release candidate and on its release alike, so it's .NET's, not new in GA. A user's host that exited as the IDE began reading it left a thread spinning and the host never seen as ended. | Fixed: `LocalHostProcess` passes its redirected streams through `PipeStartGuard` on Unix, which keeps their first asynchronous read or write and their disposal (the stream's, and the `Process`'s) apart. `PipeStartGuardTests` races the two 3000 times: without the guard it hung 5 of 5 runs, within 200 rounds; with it, 5 of 5 passed. |

| `RemoteShellTests.A_remote_window_opens_edits_completes_builds_runs_and_forwards_ports_through_the_server`: "Completion took 193 ms at 150 ms RTT: over the budget of 188 ms (completion through the server: 18 ms; 74 ms at 50 ms RTT; 193 ms at 150 ms RTT)" (the budget is asserted off CI only) | Linux (a cloud container), Debug, the full `Codalus.Shell.Tests` run on PR #258's second commit, 2026-10-31; it passed in the run before and 3 of 3 alone; Linux (a cloud container), Release, a full `eng/validate.sh` run (3 test modules at a time) on the .NET 11 GA SDK, 2026-11-11: "Completion took 181 ms at 50 ms RTT: over the budget of 147 ms (completion through the server: 77 ms [77, 71, 87, 249, 71]; 181 ms at 50 ms RTT [112, 193, 181, 142, 208])" | not CI | Not known yet. The budget is the no-latency median plus the RTT plus 20 ms, each a median of 5 measured one after the other, so load that comes and goes during the run (other tests' hosts and builds) falls on one measurement and not the other. `LatencyStream` delays each chunk from its arrival, so the simulated RTT doesn't add up over a reply's chunks. Again on 2026-11-11, on the .NET 11 GA SDK: there the baseline was 77 ms; alone, three runs measured 19 to 38 ms, about 67 ms at 50 ms and 175 ms at 150 ms, within the budget and as on RC3, so the GA SDK made completion no slower. Steady CPU load on every core doesn't reproduce it: the old measurement passed 5 of 5 under it. So the load has to come and go during the measurement, as in a full run. | Open. The failure now lists every sample of each median, telling one slow sample from a slower path. If it's load, measure the baseline and each RTT in alternating rounds, as the benchmarks do. Since 2026-11-11 the test measures in alternating rounds (no latency, 50 ms, 150 ms, five times), as the benchmarks do, so load that comes and goes falls on all three alike. It passed 3 of 3 alone and 3 of 3 under load. Not reproduced locally, so this stays open until full runs pass it. **Reproduced and fixed (2026-11-17)**, with the `RemoteServerTests` row below: under load that comes and goes on Windows (every core busy for 100 to 700 ms, then idle for 100 to 700 ms, beside other test runs), the alternating rounds' medians still went over in 4 of 10 runs ("Completion took 111 ms at 50 ms RTT: over the budget of 111 ms (completion through the server: 41 ms [39, 168, 58, 29, 41]; 111 ms at 50 ms RTT [153, 92, 111, 286, 74] …)"). Load only adds time, and adds more to a longer request: a 150 ms completion overlaps more of a burst than a 20 ms one, so the medians drift apart in any order. Fix: each is the fastest of nine alternating rounds, which every round trip the protocol makes still adds to: 0 of 10 over under the same load. **The fix didn't hold under heavier load (2026-11-19):** on a machine oversubscribed for minutes (other agents' test runs and builds beside this one, under load that comes and goes on every core; the Git Repository flows test took 128 s instead of 40), the fastest still went over in 2 of 18 runs beside five other tests ("Completion took 147 ms at 50 ms RTT: over the budget of 133 ms (completion through the server: 63 ms [115, 116, 63, 178, 110, 151, 159, 138, 93]; 147 ms at 50 ms RTT [269, 192, 176, 236, 238, 166, 186, 319, 147]; 245 ms at 150 ms RTT [277, 346, 266, 323, 289, 353, 245, 262, 329])") and in 4 of 5 runs alone. Noise, not round trips: the baseline's own samples spread over 100 ms, the excess was about the same at 50 and 150 ms, and in one run the fastest at 150 ms was only 33 ms above the fastest at 50 ms, which no protocol cost does. A baseline measured through the latency simulator at 1 ms was no different from 0 ms, so the simulator's timer isn't it. **Fix:** the budget is ADR 0002's for latency against its own noise (amendment 2026-10-27): over by 20 ms and by 3 σ of the baseline's samples (σ = 1.4826 × their median absolute deviation). A quiet machine keeps the 20 ms; a round trip more still adds 50 or 150 ms to every sample, so it fails whenever the run is steady enough to measure it. Replayed, the new rule passes all six of those runs, each over the old one, and the failure message gives the 3 σ. **Closed (2026-11-19).** With the noise band, 31 of 31 runs passed on Windows 11 (20 logical cores), each run one process with the tests of six rows of this ledger (EditingDepthTests' snippets, EditorTests' signature help, XamlShellTests' previewer, EverydayResponsivenessTests' Git Repository flows, RemoteShellTests' remote window, SettingsScrollTests' rows), under load that comes and goes (every core busy for 100 to 700 ms, then idle as long) beside other agents' shell test runs and builds on the same machine (a run took 2 to 8 minutes, the Git Repository flows alone up to 131 s instead of about 40). |

The runtime walks the heap at the end of any blocking GC while its forced GC is under way, and `HeapGraph` takes every walk before the induced GC's end, keeping the first node at a repeated address, so the induced walk's objects at addresses the earlier walk had would be unreached, and its edges resolve to the earlier walk's objects. A GC of the app's own that was running when the snapshot's forced GC began (it waits for it), or that ran while it waited for a background GC (Leaky's growing delegate list allocates on the large object heap), would give that. Not reproduced: 140 snapshots of Leaky locally (Windows), 40 of them after it ran 40 s, with its gen0 budget at 32 KB so it collected many times a second, each held one walk, the induced GC's, on the diagnostics thread | A diagnostic: the failure says the snapshot's objects and roots, and every referrer group of the step where the path stopped. Now also how its events fit together (`HeapSummary.Integrity`, from `HeapGraph`): each GC whose walk it holds (its number, generation, reason, type and thread), repeated addresses, the edges expected and received, root events and those at no object, unreached objects and lost events, which tells the three ways apart (`The_heap_graph_says_how_its_events_fit_together`). Open until a failure says which **Closed (2026-11-19), not reproduced, under the maintainer's rule for 1.0** (M65's stress, above): 50 of 50 passed on Linux runners in the CI stress run, and 55 of 55 on WSL. Still a node of the SDK's MSBuild dying; still not fixed. **Reasoned to its cause (2026-11-17), not reproduced; not seen since 40918787207 (2026-10-29) in about 300 runs:** all three were a node of a test fixture's own `dotnet build` (the CLI, not the IDE's build host), on the macOS runner, in `others`, where two test modules run at once and both build fixtures. The CLI's default is node reuse: a build leaves its worker nodes running, and the next MSBuild of the same version, from any process, connects to an idle one. So the fixtures' builds of two test processes shared one set of long-lived nodes, each node connected, released and taken by builds of other processes, and every failure file is of such a node: twice its pipe's read loop (`NodeEndpointOutOfProcBase.RunReadLoop`, ENOBUFS) and once its shutdown of one client's requests (`OutOfProcNode.HandleShutdown`, MSB0001). The IDE's own builds never reuse nodes (`SolutionBuilder`: `EnableNodeReuse = false`), so users of the IDE don't meet it. **Fix:** test runs disable node reuse (`MSBUILDDISABLENODEREUSE=1` in `eng/ci-test.sh` and `eng/validate.sh`), so each fixture's build has nodes of its own that end with it, as the IDE's do. Open until macOS CI has run `others` without it again through M65's five nightlies. **Closed (2026-11-19), not reproduced, under the maintainer's rule for 1.0** (M65's stress, above): 204 of 204 passed on macOS runners in the CI stress run (3 cores, 2 busy loops, its fixtures built each round). On the Mac mini (10 cores, arm64) at develop `9829a094` the method then passed 474 of 474 in each of its four cases (MSTest, xunit v2 and v3, NUnit) beside `yes` on 5 cores, another test loop and the smoke. |

TODO(issue) **Narrowed (2026-11-17), from the sources:** a program launched for netcoredbg that stays in state S for a minute without its runtime's transport is, short of not having reached its runtime at all, held in its runtime's startup handshake: the runtime posts its startup semaphore and then waits for the continue semaphore without a limit (`NotifyRuntimeUsingSemaphores`, dotnet/runtime's PAL), and its debugger transport comes only after that. dbgshim posts the continue only once netcoredbg's startup callback has returned (`PAL_RuntimeStartupHelper::InvokeStartupCallback`, dotnet/diagnostics), or when netcoredbg unregisters. So the next failure's `Describe()` (logged when the wait runs out) tells it: the program's main thread in a futex wait and a netcoredbg thread blocked is a startup callback that never returned (netcoredbg's); a program still running its host (no futex) never got that far. Still open. **Corrected (2026-11-19), from the sources:** the runtime's debugger transport comes before the startup handshake, not after it. `Debugger::Startup` (dotnet/runtime, `debug/ee/debugger.cpp`) creates the transport (`DbgTransportSession::Init`, whose channel makes the `-in` and `-out` FIFOs) and only then calls `PAL_NotifyRuntimeStarted`, which posts the startup semaphore and waits for the continue one ("This needs to be outside the lock and after the transport is initialized"); that is also the order `DebugAdapter.WaitForRuntimeAsync`'s remarks rely on. So a program in state S for a minute without the transport never reached its runtime's debugger start: it was still in the host, or its transport failed to initialize (the runtime then runs on without a debugger, and this Debuggee would have ended in a second), or it was still the suspended child dbgshim made, before its `exec` (a single thread in a pipe read, netcoredbg's image). `DebugAdapter.Describe`'s thread waits at the 60 s tell those apart. **Closed (2026-11-19), not reproduced, under the maintainer's rule for 1.0** (as above): 225 of 225 runs of `DebuggerDepthTests` (15 tests, this one among them) passed under netcoredbg (`CODALUS_DEBUG_ADAPTER=netcoredbg`) in WSL (Ubuntu, x64, 20 cores, the Release build) at develop `9314aaaf` and this branch's M65 fixes, each round at once with a debugged test run and the smoke test's debug steps (and in 125 rounds five Shell debugger tests), under a busy loop on twice the cores and at times another agent's test runs: load average 40 to 78. Not seen in CI's 97 pipeline runs from 2026-11-16 to 2026-11-19 either. |

TODO(issue). **Not recurred in the last 99 runs (2026-11-18). A third way, from the host's side:** a request handler runs on the connection's dispatch loop until its first await of something unfinished (`JsonRpcConnection.DispatchAsync` starts it inline), and every message after it, the signature help and the resolve's own cancellation among them, waits until then; a resolve whose description work never yields (a cached semantic model, synchronous symbol display) would be "the one request in progress" while the signature help was read but never dispatched. The host's reports now tell it from the other two: a request that held the loop over 2 s says so (`Request X ran N ms before its first await; the messages after it waited`), and the slow-request line counts the messages waiting to be dispatched, which it couldn't before (the channel can't count them, so the notification line always said -1). `A_request_that_holds_the_dispatch_loop_before_its_first_await_is_reported_with_the_messages_behind_it` checks both. Open until a failure says which **Closed (2026-11-19) under the maintainer's rule for 1.0**: not reproduced after a serious stress attempt with its diagnostic in place, and not reasoned to a cause: 50 of 50 runs passed on Windows 11 (20 logical cores), each run one process with the tests of six rows of this ledger (EditingDepthTests' snippets, EditorTests' signature help, XamlShellTests' previewer, EverydayResponsivenessTests' Git Repository flows, RemoteShellTests' remote window, SettingsScrollTests' rows), under load that comes and goes (every core busy for 100 to 700 ms, then idle as long) beside other agents' shell test runs and builds on the same machine (a run took 2 to 8 minutes, the Git Repository flows alone up to 131 s instead of about 40). No failure of it in the last 99 pipeline runs either. One of the ways this wait could fail, signature help closed by a sideways scroll, is gone with the `EditingDepthTests` snippet row's fix. It reopens if it fails again; its diagnostic stays for that. |

(`EntityFrameworkIntegrationTests.cs:217`), in 3.8 s | Linux, a full `eng/validate.sh` run (Release, 3 test modules at a time) for the Avalonia 12.1.6 update, which the Projects tests don't use | local validation, 2026-11-10 | Not known yet. After the solution closed, one of the build host's children that started with the command was still running 300 ms later. It passed alone 5 of 5. Two causes fit: a `dotnet ef` the host's exit killed (`DotnetTools.KillAll`) but that wasn't reaped yet (`Process.HasExited` can't tell a zombie from a running process), or another new child of the host that it doesn't track and end. | Diagnostic: the failure now names each process left, with its state (Z for a zombie), its parent and its command line on Linux. `TODO(issue)` in the test. **Reasoned (2026-11-17), not reproduced:** the test counts the host's new direct children, and for this fixture (one project, so design-time builds run in the host's own node, `DesignTimeBuilder.NodeCount`) that is only the `dotnet` that runs `dotnet ef`; a child the host doesn't track is out. The host kills it with its tree (`DotnetProcess.KillAll`) and returns from `Main` at once. A killed child stays a zombie until its parent reaps it, which .NET does when its SIGCHLD handling gets to it; a host that exits first leaves it to the process that adopts it, which in a container whose first process isn't an init never reaps, and the test (`Process.HasExited` of another process) counts a zombie as running: "in 3.8 s" fits one that stayed. Not reproduced: idle and under load (WSL, 8 children), .NET reaped them before the check. **Hardening:** the host waits for each child it killed, up to 5 s in all, which reaps it, before it exits (`ChildProcesses.KillAndReap`); `ChildProcessesTests` checks that none is left, zombie included, when the kill returns (it doesn't fail without the wait on an idle machine). Open until a loaded Linux validation runs the class again: the diagnostic names any process left and its state. **Still open (2026-11-19), half of the reasoning ruled out where validations run:** in WSL (Ubuntu, where `eng/wsl-test.sh` runs `eng/validate.sh`; systemd is its first process), a child that was killed and left unreaped by a parent that then exited was gone at the first look after the exit: orphaned zombies are reaped there at once, so an unreaped zombie can't have outlived the host by 300 ms in WSL, only on a Linux without a reaping init (a container), and where that run was isn't recorded. What's left for WSL: a killed `dotnet ef` still ending 300 ms after the host's exit (a process killed while it waits on memory, in a run short of it as the `ShellMatrixTests` row's below was), or the host's own end taking over the supervisor's 2 s (`SupervisedProcess.DisposeAsync`) while it waits up to 5 s for its children (`ChildProcesses.KillAndReap`), so the shell's tree kill, which doesn't wait, ends it instead. Not reproduced; the diagnostic tells them apart (a process's state: D or R, not Z). **Closed (2026-11-19), not reproduced, under the maintainer's rule for 1.0** (M65's stress, above): 50 of 50 passed on Linux runners in the CI stress run, and 55 of 55 on WSL. The process diagnostic stays. |

| `IdeExecutionServerTests.Disposal_ends_the_sessions_and_closes_a_listening_notification_socket_without_waiting_for_it`: `TimeoutException` at the disposal's 10 s (`IdeExecutionServerTests.cs:86`); the state then "sessions []; notification sockets [Aborted]; … disposed" | Windows `others`, PR #300's run (a pipeline-only change: nothing near it); Windows `others`, the AI Console pull request (nothing near Aspire), 2026-11-12 | 41849511374, 43184717554; reproduced in 43192618601 (the `validate` branch, the test below alone) | Not known yet. The runner was starved as the test ran (`[load]` 15:03:15: CPU 99%, 21 threads waiting for a CPU, a disk queue of 10). Disposal's teardown has two bounded waits of 5 s (the lifetime's cancellation, which runs the sockets' pending reads' callbacks that an `HttpListener` WebSocket on Windows can block in, and the accept loop's end) and an unbounded `HttpListener.Close` between them, so both waits running out, or a slow close, pass the test's 10 s without the server waiting for the peer, which is what the test is about; the socket was already aborted, and nothing said which step took the time. **Found (2026-11-12).** A test that repeats the race (`Disposal_never_blocks_while_sessions_end_and_orchestrators_listen`: three listening orchestrators, three sessions ending as the server is disposed) failed in its 23rd round on Windows with the stacks of both threads. On Windows, `HttpListener`'s WebSocket links each operation to the token it is given (`OutstandingOperationHelper`) and, when the token is cancelled, runs the link's callback and the operation's continuation inside the cancellation; the continuation aborts the socket, which takes the socket's locks. `WebSocketBase.Abort` holds those locks while it disposes the links (`CleanUp`), and disposing a link waits for its callback when another thread is running it. The server gave the socket's read and its sends the lifetime token. A session's `sessionTerminated`, sent as disposal cancelled that token, failed, aborted the socket on its thread and waited in `Linked1CancellationTokenSource.Dispose` for the read's callback; the cancelling thread was in that callback (`OnCancel` → `TrySetCanceled` → `ReceiveAsyncCore` → `Abort`), waiting in `TakeLocks`. Neither returns: a deadlock, not a slow step, which users can hit when they stop an AppHost. Why the listener's close then also took over 5 s isn't known; it was seen only with those two threads blocked. | A diagnostic: the server records when disposal reached each step of its teardown (sessions stopped, teardown started, cancelled or its 5 s ran out, listener closed, accept loop ended or its 5 s ran out, sockets and connections aborted), in the failure's state (`DescribeState`) and in the log line of a teardown left behind (`The_state_lists_when_disposal_reached_each_step_of_its_teardown` checks it). `TODO(issue)` in `IdeExecutionServer`. **The diagnostic named the step in 43184717554:** "teardown 10.0 s in: sessions stopped at 0.0 s, teardown started at 0.0 s, the cancellation's 5 s ran out at 5.0 s", and no "listener closed" after it. So the lifetime's cancellation blocked for its whole 5 s (the pending read's callback, in the socket's abort), and then `CloseListener` (`HttpListener.Close`, the one unbounded step) hadn't returned 5 s later: the close waits behind the same blocked callback. The runner wasn't starved this time (`[load]` 08:59:39: CPU 3%, nothing waiting for a CPU), so it is the socket on Windows, not load. The test allows 10 s; the server itself gives the teardown 15 s before leaving it behind, so the test fails before the server would have let go. Still to do (`TODO(issue)` in `IdeExecutionServer`): what blocks the read's cancellation callback on Windows, and whether the listener should be aborted instead of closed once the cancellation has run out. **Fix (2026-11-12):** the notification sockets' reads and sends take no token and continue on the thread pool; disposal ends them by aborting each socket, then its connection, then closing the listener, and a socket accepted during disposal ends itself. `BrowserRefreshServer`, which gave its pages' sockets tokens the same way, changed with it. The repeating test stays (on Windows it fails without the fix, run 43192618601, and passes with it, run 43192664617), both tests fail when a step of the teardown runs out of its time, and `TestWatchdog.ManagedStacks` gives a test the stacks of every thread at the moment it catches something blocked. The test's 10 s stays under the server's 15 s on purpose, so a disposal can't pass on the server's fallback. The `TODO(issue)` is narrowed to the listener's close. **Closed (2026-11-17):** the slow close was seen once, only while the two deadlocked threads held the socket's locks, and the fix ended that deadlock. The repeating test fails when a disposal, the close included, takes over 3 s; it passed in every pipeline run since its fix (no failure of it in the 180 runs from 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-17, 78 of them with Windows' `others` shard passing), and in 10 local runs on Windows 11 under load that comes and goes (300 disposals each). The `TODO(issue)` is gone from `IdeExecutionServer`. |

| `smoke (ubuntu-latest)`: `blazor.compoundHotReload.netcoredbg`, "Timed out after 180 s waiting for: the server refuses the update under the debugger" | Linux, develop's dry run (1 of a streak) on 9f711dc5, 2026-11-21 | 44952519485 | The step's server (18509, under `dotnet watch`) didn't start: Kestrel failed with "Failed to bind to address http://127.0.0.1:36037: address already in use", and netcoredbg stopped at that unhandled exception. A user pressing Stop and F5 on a web app hits the same. | **Fixed in the product:** `DebugService.StopAsync` waits until a program it launched on this machine has ended (not behind a path mapping, whose process ids are another system's), and ends it with its process tree if it still runs 5 s after the debugger ended it; the Debug Console says so, so a recurrence shows. `DebugStopTests` (the scripted adapter answering the disconnect and leaving the program running) fails without it. And the smoke step checks that its first stop is the breakpoint, so a server that didn't start fails there with the exception's description. |
| `packages (macos-latest, osx-arm64)`: `blazor.compoundDebug`, "The step didn't finish within 12 minutes." | macOS, the packaged app's smoke test, develop's dry run on 9f711dc5, 2026-11-21, the step's first failure in 150 runs | 44955780797 | Under the Codalus debugger the server stopped at the step's breakpoint (`Counter.razor`, line 21), and the step's Continue asked "An edit can't be applied while the program runs: Updating the type of method requires restarting the application" (ENC0009), a question nobody answers in a smoke run; Continue waits for it, so the step waited until its 12 minutes ran out. Nothing was edited: the IDE's local history has no save in the BlazorHosted copy, and the step edits nothing. So Roslyn's Edit and Continue saw a change against the session's baseline that no edit made. A lead, not a finding: the Build Host's last design-time build of BlazorHosted's projects (after F5's build) said "inputs changed" here, where a passing Linux run (44952519485) took them from the cache, so the C# host may have got the projects again during the session; the log has no times to say when, and the rude edit's location wasn't logged. | Not known yet. **The diagnostic:** the Debug Console's "need a restart" line names each rude edit's file and line (`DebugHotReloadTests` checks it), and the step now fails within a minute when Continue asks, with the question and the Debug Console, instead of after 12. Open until a failure says which file Roslyn saw changed. |
| `BlazorWebAppHotReloadDebugTests.A_rude_edit_while_stopped_on_the_server_restarts_the_app_and_debugging_follows_it`: "The old server (process 51737) still runs. Debugger: Stopped; dotnet watch's session: Attached; the wait for the server saw: candidate none …; the status bar: 'Hot Reload: the app exited'" | Linux `others`, PR #373's run (Microsoft.Data.Sqlite 11: nothing near the test), 2026-11-14 | 43636497934 | Not the restart: the server's **first** start failed. dotnet watch's output has, before any edit, "Hosting failed to start … Failed to bind to address http://127.0.0.1:39719: address already in use", the debugger then stopped on that exception (the Debug Console's "Exception thrown: 'IOException'"), and held there it never ended: the rude edit's restart couldn't end it, and the session never left it ("Attached"). The port came from the test's `FreePort()`, a listener on port 0: the OS picks those from the ephemeral range (Linux 32768–60999), which outgoing connections draw their local ports from too, and the server bound it seconds later, after a restore and a build. Shown locally: a listener can't bind a port an open outgoing connection uses as its local port, with SO_REUSEADDR or without ("Address already in use" both ways). | Fixed in the test: the port comes from `TestPorts.Free()` (`tests/Shared`), a random free loopback port between 20000 and 32000, below every OS's ephemeral range. The other tests that pick a port with a port-0 listener and start a server on it later (`HttpFileTests`, `IdeExecutionServerTests`, `WatchedServerSessionTests`, `MockNuGetServer`, `MonoDebuggerTests`) can hit the same. **Done since (2026-11-17):** they take their port from `TestPorts.Free()`, and so do the shell's tests that write a port into a launch profile (`WebTests`, `BlazorTests`, `BlazorWasmTests`, `CompoundDebugTests`, `IdeToolsShellTests`); `TestPorts` is compiled into every test project (`tests/Directory.Build.props`). The test skips without Chrome locally, so CI proves it. |
| `EditorLargeTextTests.Hidden_editors_stop_their_grammar_thread_and_grammars_are_read_once`: "Assert.Contains() Failure: Item not found", `constant.numeric.json` not in the scopes at `{ "a": 1 }`'s number (line 867), which were its key's: `source.json meta.structure.dictionary.json string.json support.type.property-name.json` | Linux `shell-1`, PR #328 (dependency updates, nothing near TextMate) | 43168967815 | TextMateSharp 2.0.3, which AvaloniaEdit.TextMate 12.0.0 brings. Each editor tokenizes from two threads at once: its model's worker tokenizes the document, and AvaloniaEdit.TextMate tokenizes the lines in view on the UI thread at each scroll, both through the model's one `Tokenizer` and the editor's grammar. 2.0.3 serialized neither: the `Tokenizer`'s `DecodeMap` (which merges a token into the last when their types match) and the grammar's rule state are shared, so a line came out with tokens merged or mis-scoped. Here the line's first tokenization, as the editor opened, merged the tokens after the key's name into it, and the test's first look at the tokens, after the tab was hidden, found them. Users see it as wrong colors on a line until it's edited. Not reproduced through the test (30 runs alone, 40 beside four busy loops), but directly: one line tokenized from two threads on one tokenizer, 20,000 rounds, came out wrong in 27,882 of 40,000 results and threw in 2,898 (`KeyNotFoundException` in `DecodeMap.GetToken`); with a tokenizer each on the shared grammar, wrong in 19,013. TextMateSharp 2.0.5 (2026-11-03) locks the `DecodeMap` around each decode, serializes tokenization per grammar, and doesn't mark a line valid that was edited while it was tokenized: 0 wrong of 40,000 either way. | Fixed: the shell references TextMateSharp and TextMateSharp.Grammars 2.0.5 directly, above AvaloniaEdit.TextMate's 2.0.3. `GrammarTokenizerThreadTests` tokenizes one line from two threads on one tokenizer, 500 rounds: with 2.0.3 it failed at round 3, with 2.0.5 it passes. 2.0.5 brought one regression of its own, which the shell's full run found: a worker stopped just after it takes a line off the model's queue drops that line, so an editor hidden and shown as it opened (Dock does that) kept a running tokenizer, every line waiting and no colors (`LanguageTests.Files_get_their_language_and_grammar_colors` failed 5 of 10 runs alone, its new failure message showing the lines' state). Showing an editor now queues its first waiting line again (`CodeEditorController.QueueFirstWaitingLine`): 15 of 15 passed. |
| `HeapRootPathsTests.A_root_on_a_stack_names_its_frame_and_a_delegate_its_method`: "no root path to a Codalus.Shell.Tests.HeapRootPathsTests+Held in the snapshot (5.0 s)" at `HeapRootPathsTests.cs:47` | Linux `shell-1` (43368524423); Windows `shell-1`, PR #346's second run (the CI workflow and a smoke test wait: nothing near the test); Linux `shell-1`, PR #351's run (the save-changes dialog: nothing near the test), 2026-11-13; Windows `shell-1`, PR #368's run (MySqlConnector 3.0: nothing near the test), 2026-11-14, after the fix below | 43368524423, 43422861486, 43447015536, 43583064438 | The snapshot was taken when it couldn't show the holding thread's roots. **On CI, a GC under way:** the diagnostic's first failure (43447015536) said "0 Codalus.Shell.Tests.HeapRootPathsTests+Held on the heap, 0 of them picked; the heap can't be walked (a GC was under way); 21 managed thread(s), 1 running managed code, one of them the GC's; thread 14 (Preemptive, state 0x2021200) has 13 stack root(s), at … ManualResetEventSlim.Wait < HeapRootPathsTests.HoldOnStack …", after 15.1 s: the holding thread was blocked as it should be (preemptive, its roots reported), and the snapshot was taken while another test of the same process (the test host at 1.3 GB) collected; a heap that can't be walked lists no objects at all. Reproduced locally (Linux): with a thread allocating beside it, 1 of 25 snapshots caught a GC that way. **And a second way, reproduced locally:** a snapshot that catches the holding thread still running managed code (cooperative: the end of `holding.Set()`, the spin before `ManualResetEventSlim.Wait` blocks) has none of its frames' roots, as its stack may not be walked (25 of 25 snapshots of a thread in a `Thread.SpinWait` loop: "0 stack root(s), at DebuggerU2MCatchHandlerFrame"). Quiet, with the thread blocked: 0 of 75. **A third way, on Windows, after the fix (43583064438):** "1 … Held on the heap, 1 of them picked; the heap can be walked; 20 managed thread(s), 1 running managed code; thread 20 (Preemptive, state 0x202b200) has 0 stack root(s), at Interop+Kernel32.WaitForMultipleObjectsEx < Interop+Kernel32.WaitForMultipleObjectsEx": the holder was stopped in its wait, as the fix expects, but its stack walked no further than the wait's two P/Invoke frames, so none of its callers' frames, `HoldOnStack`'s included, gave their roots. | A snapshot that can't show the roots is set aside and another taken (`HeapRootPaths.Describe`, up to 10), and the answer says how many were set aside and why: one taken while a GC is under way (`CanWalkHeap` false), or while the holder thread, which a caller now names (`holder:`, a managed thread id; the test names its thread), runs managed code (#352). With a thread allocating beside it, 0 of 100 failed and 3 snapshots were set aside for a GC; a thread spinning in managed code was found once a snapshot caught it walkable. A thread that never stops running still has no roots after 10 snapshots, and the answer says so. The diagnostic (308aa41d) stays: a search with no path says how many of the type are on the heap and were picked, whether the heap can be walked, the threads running managed code, and the holder thread's GC mode, state, stack roots and frames. After 43583064438, a snapshot whose holder thread has no stack roots is set aside too (its first frames go in the reason), since the holder's own frame holds the object. Not reproduced on Linux, where the waiting thread's stack walks through (10 of 10 runs of the class pass); open until Windows CI passes it. **Closed (2026-11-17):** Windows CI passed it in every run since the fix (d20a2873, 2026-11-14): `test (windows-latest, shell-1)` passed in 26 runs from 2026-11-16 to 44128943909, the nightly 44042755897 among them (its 2 cancelled ones were cancelled by other jobs), and the last 100 runs' failures (`eng/ci-failures.sh --recent 100`, 43733266567 to 44177540699) don't have it |

Reasoned from the code: the scripted adapter kills its program (`TerminateProcess`, at once) and only then reports its exit, so by the check the program had gone; the check found a process by the program's id only (`Process.GetProcessById`). Windows gives a freed process id to the next process that starts, and the runner was starting processes all the while (Chromium, test hosts), so another process had the id. The other cause the row named, a killed program taking more than 5 s to go, is the less likely: the kernel ends a terminated process's threads without running them, and the program had been killed well before the check. The same check in the class's `Dispose` would also have killed that other process | Each program's line in the programs file has its start time (`ScriptedDap.StartProgram`, the test's own launches: `ProgramLine`), and the check passes over a process with the program's id that started at another time (`TookItsId`, a second's leeway). `The_check_for_programs_left_running_tells_a_program_from_another_process_with_its_id` lists a running process with another start time, which the check without the fix took for a program left, and with its own, which it still finds. | `WatchedServerSessionTests.The_probe_reaches_an_app_listening_on_only_one_of_localhosts_addresses`: `Assert.True() Failure` at the probe of `127.0.0.1` (line 426), with a listener on it, right after the probe of `localhost` passed | macOS all, the nightly | 43686325192 | The product's: `WatchedServer.AcceptsAsync` gives one attempt 500 ms, which bounded the IDE's own scheduling too. The IDE's tools that ask once (`ShellHost.IdeTools`) would say so too. | Fixed: the probe's connects are the OS's own (non-blocking, waited for with `Socket.Select`, the error read from the socket), and `localhost` is the loopback addresses with no lookup. A first fix, which asked the OS about the runtime's own connects once the attempt was up, read a refused connect as made on Linux (the runtime had taken its error already); `Without_the_agents_id_or_a_listening_line_the_tree_and_a_probe_find_it` caught it (#396's run 43729362959). With a busy pool: 0 wrong answers in 187,390 probes of a listening and a closed port. `A_connection_the_os_made_counts_though_the_attempt_was_up_before_its_completion_ran` (an attempt of no time) fails without it. |
| `SettingsScrollTests.Opening_all_settings_makes_the_rows_near_the_view_and_the_rest_in_the_background_without_moving_the_view`: "Values are not within tolerance 1, Expected: -60, Actual: -8" at its last assertion (`SettingsScrollTests.cs:112`). The setting at the top of the view was 52 px lower once the rows above it were made, as if the scroll viewer's anchoring hadn't made up for them | Windows shell-1, #396 (a change to the server probe only) | 43733266567 | **Found (2026-11-19):** the scroll viewer makes up for the rows above the view only when it arranges (`ScrollContentPresenter.ArrangeWithAnchoring`), and it arranges only when its content's height changes. When the rows made in one background turn above the view grow by as much as those made below it shrink (each row stood in at its template's average height), the list's height stays, so only the list's panel is measured and arranged again, on its own: the rows move and the anchoring never runs, and what's shown moves by what the rows above grew. A slice that finishes the rows above and starts those below has a sum of zero rarely, which is why a sweep of 400 jumps saw it twice. (Before: the test passed 6 of 6 runs alone on Linux.) Each `DeferredContent` row is an anchor candidate of the scroll viewer; the row the viewer anchors to, and whether it changed while the rows above were made, isn't in the log. | Diagnostic: the assertion now says where the row was and is, with the view's offset, extent and viewport and how many rows were made, before and after. Open until the cause is found. **Reproduced locally (Windows, 2026-11-17), rarely:** a sweep of 80 jumps (8 window sizes, 10 places in All Settings, the test's steps each time) moved the row twice, at 800×800 half way down (-37 to 3 px) and at 81 % (0 to -16 px), in one sweep of five. Both times the row's own height stayed (79 px), and at half way the next row was further from the view's top (42 px) than this one, so Avalonia's anchor (the candidate whose top-left is nearest the viewport's, picked again at every arrange: `ScrollContentPresenter.ArrangeWithAnchoring`) should have been this row. Ruled out: a stale anchor (the presenter marks it dirty after every arrange, so making each row re-register changed nothing), and the rows' heights. Open: whether the anchoring didn't run for the change (the rows arranged without the presenter) or something else set the offset. A second diagnostic: the failure also gives the scroll viewer's anchor before and after, and every change of the offset while the rows above are made, with the row's place, the anchor and its callers. **Closed (2026-11-19).** Fix: a row that is made measures again everything up to its scroll viewer (`DeferredContent.Build`), so the scroll viewer arranges with it and its anchoring makes up for the rows above whatever the list's height does. `SettingsScrollTests.A_row_above_that_grows_as_much_as_one_below_shrinks_does_not_move_the_view` makes the 7th row 40 px taller and the 22nd 40 px shorter in one background turn: the row at the top of the view moved 40 px without the fix, and stays with it. With the fix, 50 of 50 runs passed on Windows 11 (20 logical cores), each run one process with the tests of six rows of this ledger (EditingDepthTests' snippets, EditorTests' signature help, XamlShellTests' previewer, EverydayResponsivenessTests' Git Repository flows, RemoteShellTests' remote window, SettingsScrollTests' rows), under load that comes and goes (every core busy for 100 to 700 ms, then idle as long) beside other agents' shell test runs and builds on the same machine (a run took 2 to 8 minutes, the Git Repository flows alone up to 131 s instead of about 40). |

The list keeps the first 500 (`MaxFailures`, until the IDE takes them), so in a whole run it was full before this test logged its own, which were dropped; and its wait for the `bindings` event was met at once by the failures already there. Proven: the test with 600 other failures logged first fails without the change below, every time. The product keeps the 500 for the IDE's Error List, which takes them on that event. | The test takes what's already there before its window opens, then takes until both of its own failures came; its named element has a name of its own (a failure is reported once per name, property and path for the process's life); a theory case logs 600 other failures first. **Closed (2026-11-19).** |
| `git init -q -b main` failed with no message in 41 `Codalus.Shell.Tests` tests (every test that starts git from about 40 minutes into the module: `SolutionExplorerGitGlyphsTests`, `DiffDocumentTests`, `GitShellTests`, `GitDepthShellTests`, `HostingShellTests`, `IdeToolsGitShellTests`, `GitChangesRowsTests`, `GitChangesMenuTests`, `SettingsSyncTests`, `CodeLensDetailsTests` and others), with in the same run `HeapRootPathsTests` (2: no root path in the snapshot's first 61 s), `IconThemeTests.The_setting_offers_a_plugins_icon_theme_and_switches_the_file_icons` ("Timed out waiting for the shell") and `DebugHotReloadTests.A_process_attached_to_without_modifiable_assemblies_says_why_edits_dont_apply` (its status `null`, not "waiting") | Windows, the 1.0 release candidate's validation (develop `9314aaaf`, 2026-11-18 23:52 to 01:44), the whole solution, 3 modules at a time, with another session's `Codalus.Shell.Tests` run on the machine (CPU about 91 %), shortly after C: had filled and WSL had been restarted | (local) | Not known. Every one starts a child process, so starting processes from that test process went wrong from some point on. Ruled out: the process's environment (read from its memory: only the expected `CODALUS_*` and `HISTFILE` beyond its parent's, `PATH` the same), its handles (7132) and threads (41), and memory (no resource-exhaustion event in the System log; commit limit 71.8 GB). The git helpers didn't print git's exit code, so the failures said nothing more. The whole module again, with no other test run on the machine (1201 tests): every one of them passed (one other test failed, the `LiveXamlTests` row above). | Diagnostic: the 15 git helpers of `Codalus.Shell.Tests` say git's exit code (decimal and hex: a process that never started reports an NTSTATUS such as `0xC0000142`). The stress ran those tests, not the whole solution the failure came from. The exit codes' diagnostic stays. |
