## Smoke test

| Failure | Where | Runs | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `debug.depth` (the Codalus debugger): "Timed out after 30 s waiting for: inline values show args after its line", after Run to Cursor had stopped at both lines and QuickWatch and the PerfTip had passed | WSL (Ubuntu, 20 cores), the debug steps alone (`CODALUS_SMOKE_ONLY`) of the Release (JIT) shell at develop `9314aaaf` and its M65 fixes, in a stress loop beside the depth tests, a debugged test run and Shell debugger tests, a busy loop on twice the cores and at times another agent's test runs: load average 40 to 78; 8 times in 175 runs | not CI | The product's, in `CodeEditorController.LoadInlineValuesAsync`: a cancellation and an answer racing. A stop raises a few debugger events within milliseconds (the state, the selected frame), and each refreshes the inline values, cancelling the one before. A refresh whose answer came after its cancellation still cleared what was drawn on its way out, so when the newest refresh had already drawn the values, the one it cancelled wiped them, and nothing drew them again for that stop. The diagnostic's history (below) showed it: three refreshes of the stop within 30 ms, the last drew `args`'s line, the one it cancelled answered after it and cleared it. A user saw the values vanish for that stop. | **Fixed (2026-11-19):** a cancelled refresh leaves what is drawn to the newer one (a failed one too). `InlineValuesTests.A_stop_draws_each_lines_values_and_marks_the_ones_a_step_changed` holds a superseded refresh's answer until the newer one has drawn: it fails without the fix ("The superseded refresh cleared the newer one's values"). The diagnostic stays: the editor keeps its last refreshes (`InlineValuesOutcome`: why one asked nothing, the values named and on how many lines, the frame's variables, what was drawn, what failed or was cancelled), which the wait's failure says. 50 of 50 runs passed after the fix in the same loop. |
| `debug.depth` (the Codalus debugger): "Expected: QuickWatch evaluates args[1]: ." (QuickWatch empty, with no error) | WSL, the same stress loop; once in 175 runs | not CI | The smoke test's: it read QuickWatch as soon as `ShowQuickWatchAsync` returned. The debugger posts the stop's own refresh of the window when it raises the stop, just after its state says stopped; under load that refresh ran after the window was shown and the call had asked, so the call's answer gave way to the newer one (`QuickWatchViewModel`'s generation), which came a moment later. Not a user's problem: the window shows the value then. | **Fixed (2026-11-19):** the step waits for the value, and its failure says what QuickWatch shows. 50 of 50 runs passed after it in the same loop. |
| `debug.session` (the Codalus debugger): "Expected: the session reports the exit", the session idle after Continue with no "exited with code" in the Debug Console | WSL, the same stress loop as the rows above; once in 225 runs | not CI | Not known yet: the check didn't print the Debug Console. The family of `DebugServiceTests.Logpoints_write_messages_without_stopping` in the Tests table (an adapter's `exited` lost), whose Codalus-debugger cause was fixed on 2026-11-17 (`DapServer.SendEndAsync`); if the Debug Console says "The debugger ended the session without reporting the program's exit code", that fix didn't hold. | A diagnostic: the check's failure carries the Debug Console's end. Not seen again in the 145 runs after it. **Closed (2026-11-19) under the maintainer's rule for 1.0:** reproduced once, before its diagnostic, and its cause isn't known; with the diagnostic in place it passed 145 of 145 under heavy load. It reopens if it fails again, and the failure then shows the Debug Console. |

Since 41374588224 the log's folder is made absolute (`DebugAdapterStartInfo.Local`), and `DebugAdapter.Describe` says on Linux where each thread of netcoredbg and of the program waits in the kernel (`/proc/<pid>/task/*/wchan`, with the threads' names), which tells a FIFO open, a futex and a pipe read apart. **Closed (2026-11-19), not reproduced, under the maintainer's rule for 1.0** (a row neither reproduced nor reasoned to its cause closes after 50 passes under heavy load with its diagnostic in place, and reopens if it fails again): the debug steps alone (`CODALUS_SMOKE_ONLY`: the session, depth and compound steps under each debugger), 225 runs in WSL (Ubuntu, x64, 20 cores; the Release build's shell, not the packages' Native AOT one) at develop `9314aaaf` and this branch's M65 fixes, each round at once with the debugger depth tests and a debugged test run (and in 125 rounds five Shell debugger tests), under a busy loop on twice the cores and at times another agent's test runs: load average 40 to 78. What failed in that loop was new and is in the rows at the top of this table: inline values cleared by a superseded refresh (fixed), QuickWatch read too soon (fixed) and one missing exit report (open). That's x64: no arm64 Linux machine was at hand, and the failures were the arm64 packages'. Its sibling on macOS (`DebuggerDepthTests` and `LateRuntimeTests`, netcoredbg's same stall) closed under the same rule on 2026-11-19. |

The shell uses the default GC (workstation, concurrent) and nothing calls `GC.Collect`, so a blocking pause of over a second points to the machine's memory (WSL's VM beside it) or to a large compacting collection; the sweep records only the total pause, not the collection's generation, whether it compacted, the heap's size or the memory load. | Open, with the diagnostic: a run whose GC pauses add up to 100 ms says which collections they were (`GcReport`: how many of each generation, and of the last of each kind the runtime keeps, the longest: its generation, whether it compacted, the heap, and the memory load against what's available), in the stall's message and in `command-sweep.json`'s `gc`. `TODO(issue)`. **Still open (2026-11-19).** Not stress-run: it needs the smoke test on a Native AOT build, which the M65 pass on this row didn't run. It reopens if the report shows a long blocking gen2 under the high mark. |

| Smoke `shell.commandSweep`: (scenario) [Debug] "Start Debugging didn't stop at a breakpoint after the commands before it, and did once the solution was reopened: the debugger didn't stop at line 4 (the program ran to its end; …; breakpoints: Program.cs:1 no state; …)" | Windows (local, Native AOT), the 1.0 validation (2026-11-20) | not CI | In the product. The breakpoint set on line 4 was on line 1 when the program ran. The sweep's `git checkout -- src/Hello` (an Explorer command before it had removed the folder) wrote `Program.cs` with CRLF, under the machine's `core.autocrlf=true` and no `.gitattributes` in the fixture's repository, over the editor's LF text, and the reload replaced the text from line 1's end to the file's end as one change: every anchor in it, the debug margin's for each breakpoint (and bookmarks and pinned data tips), moved to line 1. A user whose checkout, pull or formatter changed a file's line endings lost every breakpoint's line in it the same way. | Fixed: a reload's change keeps the lines whose text stayed at either end of it and replaces only their line breaks (`CodeDocument.Edits`, one undoable change through `DocumentEdits`, so the language service still gets one edit). `EditorLargeTextTests.A_reload_of_new_line_endings_keeps_each_breakpoint_on_its_line` (CRLF to LF, LF to CRLF, a last line without a break) failed 3 of 3 without it, every anchor on line 1. |
| Smoke `shell.commandSweep`: `help.setUp [menu]` "the UI thread stalled for 979 ms (… the GC paused 126 ms …: memory load 80% of 32602 MB …; it ran no posted job (8 of 8 samples))", `editor.action.triggerSuggest [key Ctrl+Space]` 551 ms (GC 0 ms) and `workbench.navigateBack [menu]` 502 ms (GC 46 ms), each in no posted job | Windows (local, Native AOT), the 1.0 validation (2026-11-20), the smoke alone after the suite | not CI | Not known yet. CI's Windows smoke on the same code the same day (run 44601863423) took 110, 0 and 6 ms for them; the stalls came minutes apart, early in the sweep, with nothing else of the validation running and the PC's memory load near 80%. "No posted job" leaves layout, rendering, input or a command's own code, or a UI thread that wasn't given a core, and the report couldn't tell which. | **Closed (2026-11-20), reasoned to its causes** (below; neither is Codalus's code), with the diagnostics in place: the sweep's stall says how much of the longest wait the UI thread ran on a core and how busy the machine's cores were (Windows, Linux). **Read (2026-11-20), with that diagnostic:** the published app's sweep alone on the same PC, at #491's head, stalled nine other commands for 516 to 1105 ms (`git.pull`, `debug.attach`, `build.buildProject`, most on their first run), and the UI thread ran 0 to 16 ms of each: it waited, it didn't work, with the machine's cores 60 to 93% busy (another session's simulation on the PC) and its memory load at 81%. The next sweep, 20 minutes later on the same build plus the page-fault count, passed: 991 runs, no stall, in 320 s against 452. The waits come and go with the machine, not with a command. Still open: the next stall's page faults say whether the wait was code paged back in from disk (Windows trims a loaded PC's working sets) or something else. **WSL, 2026-11-20** (the final tree's smoke): `editor.action.replace [menu]` 764 ms and `run.selectLaunchProfile [menu]` 1077 ms, both on their first run, the UI thread running 0 ms of them with the machine's cores 1 and 5% busy and no major page fault: a wait on an idle machine, not the load. The sweep's samples now also name the UI thread's state in the kernel on Linux and the function it waits in, which tells the next one's wait. **50 WSL sweeps, 2026-11-20** (the published app of `develop`'s tree `41b76abf`, `CODALUS_SMOKE_ONLY=shell.commandSweep`): 48 passed; sweep 29 stalled `templates.newSolution [menu]` 759 ms and sweep 45 `file.save [menu]` 528 ms, both first runs, the UI thread asleep in `futex_wait_queue_me` at every sample, running 0 ms of it, the machine 0% busy, no major fault, in no posted job. So the sweep now reads, 300 ms into a wait asleep, the UI thread's system call and the code addresses on its stack, and every thread's into `sweep-stall-<n>.txt` (`ThreadStack`, `addr2line` on `codalus.dbg`). **The cause, its first sweep (2026-11-20):** `git.addWorktree [menu]` 897 ms. The UI thread was in Avalonia's `X11Window.DoPaint` (an Expose event), in `MediaContext.SyncCommit`, waiting (`Task.Wait`) for the compositor's render thread to present the frame; the render thread was in `XPutImage` → `_XSend` → `xcb_writev` → `poll`, waiting to write the frame into the X server's socket; every other thread slept. The X server is WSLg's Xwayland (`DISPLAY=:0`), which composites to the Windows desktop outside the distribution: it stopped reading for most of a second, which no core of the machine shows. A display server that holds a frame holds Avalonia's paint, not Codalus's code; CI's Xvfb never did (its smokes' first runs are in `SweepStallLimit`). The Windows stalls were the PC's load (above). It reopens if a stall's threads say otherwise. |
| Smoke `profiler.cpu`: "Expected: the counters were recorded." | Linux (WSL, Native AOT), the 1.0 validation (2026-11-20), with the Windows validation on the same PC | not CI | In the product. The session's `counters.json` had no counter and no runtime event, beside a CPU trace that recorded (389 KB). When the program ended, the profiler stopped its counters session, and `DiagnosticsService.StopCountersAsync` forgot the monitor before asking the host to stop: the host then sends the values and events it hasn't flushed yet and the session's end, and those notifications found no monitor. A loaded machine's host reads the stream late, so all of a 3 s run's values came in that last flush. The host's answer doesn't say they were handled either: an answer completes as it's read, while notifications wait their turn. A user profiling a short program on a busy machine got a report without counters. | Fixed: the monitor takes the host's notifications until the session's end arrives (at most 10 s after the answer). `DiagnosticsHostTests.Stopping_counters_keeps_the_values_the_host_sends_with_its_end` holds every value in the host until the stop (`CODALUS_TEST_COUNTERS_FLUSH_MS`); it failed without the fix, no value came with the stop. |

## Runners

| Failure | Where | Runs | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `curl: (56) Recv failure: Connection reset by peer` before any test of the shard ran, "Process completed with exit code 56"; the run's other jobs were cancelled | `test (macos-latest, all)`, preparing `others` after `shell-1` and `shell-2` had passed, PR #340, 2026-11-13 | 43333030374 | Infrastructure: one download of `dotnet-install.sh` (`eng/get-dotnet-x64.sh`, the x64 runtimes for Apple silicon) had its connection reset. Nothing retried it: curl without `--retry` gives up at once, and with only `--retry` it doesn't retry a reset | Every download the test, packaging and compatibility jobs make with curl (`dotnet-install.sh` in `get-dotnet-x64.sh`, `get-runtimes.sh`, `compat-containers.sh` and `validate.sh`, `get-runtimes.sh`'s OpenSSL package, `get-netcoredbg.sh`'s release) retries three times, resets included (`--retry 3 --retry-all-errors`) |
| "The action 'Test (shell-1)' has timed out after 40 minutes", with no test failed: 797 had passed and tests were still passing in its last second | `test (windows-latest, shell-1)`, the AI Console pull request (#324) on the head that merged `develop` a third time, 2026-11-12; again on `develop`'s dispatched run of 2026-11-14 and PR #387 (Solution Explorer's git glyphs, 1.5 s of tests in the shard), 2026-11-15: 824 tests passed and none failed | 43177882900, 43624505680, 43714153100 | The shard was as long as its limit on a slow runner. Its tests' own times added up to 2,400 s here, against 1,578 s for nearly the same tests (790) in that pull request's first Windows run the day before (43066231416, the step done in 26 minutes): tests that hadn't changed took half as long again or more (`EditorLargeTextTests` 41 s then, 108 s now; `BlazorWasmTests` 76 and 131; `WebToolsShellTests` 17 and 60; `EslintPrettierTests` 22 and 55), so the runner was slow, not one test. (2) `continue-on-error: ${{ steps.<smoke>.outcome == 'success' }}` on the upload (the smoke step given an id): a passed smoke whose upload failed warns, and a failed smoke's report still fails it; a release's pictures (which come from that artifact) could then be missing, so whatever reads them would have to fail on its own when the artifact isn't there. (3) a second upload step, `if: failure() && steps.<upload>.outcome == 'failure'`, a few minutes later with `overwrite: true`: a short outage (this one lasted about eleven minutes) passes, a long one still fails the run, and the pictures are always there when the run is green. What moves the strip: the Welcome plugin's Start page, which opens the left column (22% of the window) once the plugin host has started, seconds late on a busy runner, so after the test took its point; at a 900 px window that's the 205 px (41666516531's first wheel, taken from the layout before it opened, landed on its title). The point was taken once, from the strip's first layout: on Windows the strip was then 205 px further right (a tool window beside the documents), and the wheel up went to that tool window's title, in the layout as in the frame. And the wheels never reached the strip. Both went to a tool window's title (a `TextBlock` in a `ToolChromeControl`, in the docked layout's `ProportionalStackPanel`), and the shell saw none. The point is worked out from the strip's layout, but the pointer's hit test reads the last rendered frame (`ShellFixture.RenderFrame`): the tests pumped the dispatcher and never rendered a frame after opening the documents or changing the rows, so on a loaded runner the frame was older than the layout, and at the point it had something else. Locally, after the wheel down the strip is 397 px high and the wheel's point is still over a tab of its first row. The wheel's write of the setting was fire-and-forget, so a write that failed would have left the rows on without a word. Also ruled out (PR #285): the settings (every write is followed by its own reload, numbered after it, so a watcher's reload of an older state never wins), a lost wheel handler (`TabStripButtons` is attached once, before the wheels), a tooltip over the point (the tooltip service opens on pointer moves, not wheels), and the test before it (the headless tests run one at a time, each with its own application; 5 of 5 passed locally after each failing run's previous test, and a full Release `shell-1` run passed). It isn't a platform's fonts either: #280's run passed it on Windows and macOS too. Found on the way, a product race the test doesn't hit: the wheel compared with the saved setting, which changes only once the write has landed, so a wheel up right after a wheel down (a trackpad's flick and back) found one row, wrote nothing, and the rows came on. PR #287's runs are the same: in 41624195564 the point was over a tab just before the wheel down and the wheel went to a `ToolChromeControl` with the strip at x 205, because the headless window's `MouseWheel` runs pending dispatcher work before it delivers the wheel (forced locally: a layout change posted just before the wheel fails the same way), so a Start page on its way landed in between; in 41703043049 the race test's second wheel, at its first's point, was over (27, 85.5), a `ToolChromeControl`'s title. PR #293's 41701332941 failed the same way at the race test's up. All before the tests waited for the Start page. | **Fixed in the tests:** both tests wait for the Start page first (`ShellFixture.StartViewShownAsync`, as the tests that show a left-column tool window do), and each wheel goes through `Wheel`, which renders a frame first, takes its point from the strip's first tab where it is then, and fails, naming what's there, if the point isn't over the strip (the race's second wheel goes right after the first, through the same frame). The diagnostics stay in the failure messages. Users' wheels hit what's on screen. Before: The wheel's write reports a failure in the log (`[tabs]`), and the test's failure says the setting, the application resource, how many wheels reached the shell and what the last did, whether the panel is still the strip's, the strip's size and place, and what was under the wheel's point before and after. PR #285 adds, at both wheels, each wheel event's target and whether the strip saw it and the shell took it, whether the panel is attached and the overlay layer's children; `TODO(issue)` in the test. It fixes the race: while the wheel's own write is on its way, the wheel compares with what it wrote (`A_wheel_up_right_after_a_wheel_down_leaves_one_row` holds the settings' write lock across the two wheels, 3 of 3 failed without the fix). `eng/ci-failures.sh` had found nothing in either run: gh 2.102 prints no job log for a response with terminal escape sequences unless given `--allow-escape-sequences`, which the script now passes when gh knows it. The race's test now says, for its failure, what the shell did with each wheel (after the down and after the up), each wheel event and the user settings file as written: whether the up reached the shell, and whether it wrote. |

## Not flakes, but red runs

- **The benchmark regression gate** (39953872764, 39979261891, 40423773696, 40461519855): one metric just past +10% on
  a hosted runner, once with VS Code, the reference app, moving as much in the same run. That is the gate's policy on
  noisy machines (ADR 0002), a decision for the maintainer, not a test to fix here.
- **The binary-size gate, 0.12.0's release run** (41937217669): two Linux sizes over +5 % of the median of 10 runs.
  VS Code's `install_mb` (1044.6 MB over 1032.5; 1028.0 in 0.11.0's run 41352153213) failed although the reference
  application's numbers never fail: the size branch of `Gates.Compare` gated every target. Fixed: the reference
  application's sizes are reported, never failed (`GatesTests`). The shell's `shell_mb` (421.4 MB over 421.1) grew
  2.2 MB since 0.11.0's 419.2, which was already 4.5 % over the same median: the median of 10 releases trails a size
  that grows a little every release. The shipped Linux shell outside the helpers grew 1 MB (240.7 MB in 0.11.0's
  package, 241.7 MB in the 0.12.0 nightly's): the benchmarks job publishes without the packages'
  `-p:DebugType=none`, so `shell_mb` counts Native AOT's debug symbols too: a linux-x64 publish of the release commit
  as that job makes it measures 421.4 MB, CI's number, of which `codalus.dbg` is 188.7 MB (macOS keeps a `.dSYM`).
  Fixed, by the maintainer's decision: `binary-size` leaves Native AOT's symbols out as it leaves out `*.pdb`, so it
  measures what ships (232.7 MB for that local publish; ADR 0002's 2026-11-05 amendment, `HarnessTests`).
- **`LocalizationCatalogTests.The_english_catalogue_has_every_text_of_the_code_and_the_views` on macOS** (43732470763,
  43734210763, PR 398): "en.json is out of date (3 new or changed: command.ios.openSimulator, command.ios.openXcode,
  menu.menuBar/tools/ios)". Not a flake: it failed on every macOS run since the MAUI tools (ADR 0104) registered Tools →
  iOS only on a Mac, and the test built the catalogue from the registries of the OS it ran on, so `en.json`,
  regenerated on Linux, lacked them. Fixed (e54a1861): their titles go through `Localizer.Get`, as other OS-specific
  titles do, so the test finds them in the code on every OS.
- **`EnvironmentShellTests.Installing_the_runtime_from_an_update_starts_the_hosts_again_then_checks_the_update` on
  the Mac** (local validation of the release candidate, `develop` at 9314aaaf, 2026-11-18): "Timed out after 180 s:
  the install, the hosts started again and the update checked". Not a flake: the test wasn't hermetic. It described
  the machine's .NET roots to the environment check but not the machine Install .NET's options are for
  (`DescribeDotnetMachine`), so `DotnetFacts` read the real one. On a Mac whose `dotnet` is in
  `/usr/local/share/dotnet` (not under `$HOME`), the option that fits is Microsoft's `.pkg` in the terminal, which
  `InstallToolAsync` runs in a real terminal, not through the test's runner: it ran `curl` of the `.pkg` and
  `sudo installer`, and sudo waited for a password until the limit. CI passed only because setup-dotnet installs under
  `$HOME`, which makes dotnet-install, run through the runner, the one that fits; a Windows machine without winget (the
  download page, opened in the real browser) or a Linux one with a distribution's .NET could hit the same. Fixed: the
  test describes its machine, and asserts the option it installs runs through its runner; and no test can start an
  install in the terminal any more: `ShellHost.TerminalInstallStandIn`, which `ShellFixture` sets for every shell, takes
  every install meant for the terminal (the `.pkg`, a distribution's package or the snap under sudo, a workload, the
  Android SDK under sudo), records it in `ShellFixture.TerminalInstalls`, starts nothing and fails (exit code 97). The
  one test that wants the real terminal clears it and runs `exit 3`
  (`EnvironmentShellTests.A_test_never_starts_an_install_in_the_terminal` checks the guard).
- **`ProfilerToolsShellTests.Memory_usage_takes_snapshots_diffs_them_and_inspects_a_full_dump` on the Mac** (local
  validation of the release candidate, `develop` at 9314aaaf, 2026-11-18): the full dump returned null, and the test
  said only that. Not a flake: the runtime's createdump needs macOS' developer tools access
  (`system.privilege.taskport`), whose password prompt, the first time in a 10-hour window, holds every dump until it's
  answered; it wasn't. The debugger's Create Dump says so on a timeout, but the profiler's full dump showed it only in a
  toast, and the Performance Profiler's status said "The dump could not be written." Fixed: the reason is the status
  too, a timeout of the profiler's own wait says what the debugger's says (`DumpAdvice`, shared with the companion
  host), and the test's assertion prints the status and the notifications
  (`ProfilerToolsShellTests.A_full_dump_that_times_out_or_fails_says_why`).
- **`Leaky` left running after the memory test failed on the Mac** (the same validation): the test failed before its
  Stop Collection, and the program it had launched to profile outlived the test's shell, re-parented to launchd. A
  program launched to be profiled stopped only with Stop Collection (`FinishProfilingAsync`); disposing the shell
  disposed the diagnostics windows and the host, not it, so a user closing the IDE while collecting left it the same
  way. Fixed: disposing the profiling stops it as Stop Collection does (its process tree, `ProcessTree.Kill`), and the
  test ends its collection in a `finally`
  (`ProfilerToolsShellTests.A_program_launched_to_be_profiled_stops_when_the_ide_closes_during_the_collection`).
- **`finish`** (40307688648): merging master into develop conflicted beyond `VERSION`; it says so and stops.
- **The English catalogue on macOS, PR #387's merge with `develop`** (43729011051): `LocalizationCatalogTests` found
  `en.json` without `command.ios.openSimulator`, `command.ios.openXcode` and `menu.menuBar/tools/ios`. `develop`'s, on
  macOS only: PR #390 (MAUI's native tools, ADR 0104) registered Open Simulator, Open Xcode and Tools → iOS on a Mac
  only, its catalogue was made on another OS, and its own run tested Linux only (no platform path). The test wants
  every OS's catalogue the same. Fixed on `develop` (e54a1861, run 43732470763 found it too): the titles go through
  `Localizer.Get` where the commands and the submenu are registered, so the test finds them in the code on every OS,
  and its key pattern takes a menu key's slashes. PR #387 had its own fix meanwhile and took `develop`'s instead.
